ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に

―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

## Document 038: 2026年10月投資月報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月投資月報.pdf

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
利率上行引發波動，惟無損 AI 供應鏈擴張動能
2026 年 10 月
9 月全球股市普遍回落，主因為油價急升、全球長端殖利率攀高及主要央行轉鷹。僅有日本股市
受 AI、半導體需求與企業信心支撐，抵銷 BOJ 升息壓力，成為主要市場中唯一正報酬。標普 500 9
月小跌 0.3%，雖 AI 與大型科技股獲利韌性仍在，但 Fed 於 9 月升息並暗示年底前再度升息，加上
油價與美債殖利率走高，壓抑估值並引發科技股震盪。印度股市下跌 6.9%最弱，主要受油價飆升、
全球利率上行及外資單月淨流出約 27 億美元拖累。中國股市下跌 4.7%，因受內需修復偏弱、科技

股評價承壓所致；歐洲股市也因油價單月大漲近 14%與全球殖利率攀升影響，9 月下跌 4.6%。
9 月主要債市全面下跌，核心壓力來自全球殖利率急升。美歐日央行同步升息、Brent 原油單月
上漲約 14%、 美國經濟數據偏強與公債供給疑慮 ， 使美國10 年期殖利率單月上升約 53bp，創 2022
年以來最大升幅。主要券種中，全球投資級公司債下跌 2.93%表現最弱，新興美元債亦下跌 2.91%，
因 9 月全球公債殖利率快速攀升，此兩類債券對利率變化均較敏感 ； 其中投資級公司債相較高收益債
存續期較長、票息緩衝較低，因此在利率重新定價期間承受較大價格壓力。亞洲高收益債下挫 1.20%
相對抗跌，因高票息與較短存續期提供緩衝；新興當地債跌 1.74%，亦優於多數美元債種。
在股市方面，AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器 DRAM、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供
應鏈維持成長動能，市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分化。若AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資
本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整

體而言 ， 科技產業與台灣及日本股市獲利表現仍具關注度 ， 惟後續仍須留意全球景氣 、 產業供需變化 、
政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
就債市而言，雖然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶
來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率
環境下，中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級
公司債指數利差約 80 個基點 ， 雖然利差偏窄 ， 但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準 。 因此，
在目前市場環境下 ， 中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別 ， 惟後續表現仍須
留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

美國股市：通膨與升息預期上升推動美債利率上漲，美股呈高檔區間震盪
9 月因美伊關係緊張升溫 ， 推升油價並加劇市場對通膨回升的擔憂 ；相較於 6 月點陣圖 ，9 月FOMC
官員全面上修 2026 至 2027 年的政策利率預估至 4.1%，並暗示 2026 年底前會再升息一次，加上美
國經濟數據表現優於預期，使美國公債殖利率走升，進而壓抑美股表現。在此背景下，9 月標普 500
指數呈現高檔區間震盪格局。產業表現方面，資訊科技與通訊服務類股相對強勢，持續扮演指數上漲
的主要動能；相較之下，醫療保健、必需消費及部分工業類股表現較為疲弱。

9 月美國聯準會貨幣政策會議以 12 比 0 的表決結果 ， 將聯邦基金利率目標區間上調25 個基點至
3.75%到 4.00%。9 月最新經濟預測，美國聯準會官員上修 2026 年核心 PCE 至 3.4%，2027 年維持
在 2.5%，將 2026-2027 年經濟成長上調 0.1%至 2.3%與 2.4%，顯示聯準會認為美國經濟與通膨仍
具韌性。
觀察美國近期的經濟數據可以發現企業投資動能依然強健，元大投顧表示，美國8 月扣除飛機與
國防的核心資本財訂單由前期的月增 0.6%加速至 1.6%， 其中初級金屬 、 機械及電機設備訂單均呈現
增長，顯示 AI 相關投資及企業設備更新需求持續支撐資本財訂單，企業設備投資可望對經濟成長形
成穩健貢獻。
自 6 月底以來 ， 市場持續上修標普500 指數企業獲利預估 ， 其中以科技與能源類股貢獻最為明顯 ，
反映企業營運表現優於市場預期。AI 投資循環持續推進、企業數位轉型需求增加，以及美國內需維
持穩健，共同支撐企業獲利成長動能，並有助於吸收高利率環境帶來的壓力。與此同時，標普 500

指數前瞻本益比已由先前高點回落，評價壓力較先前明顯緩解。隨著企業獲利持續改善，美股行情正
由過去仰賴資金與情緒推升評價 ， 逐步轉向由基本面支撐股價 ， 後續表現將更取決於企業獲利是否持
續優於預期。
投資策略方面，投資焦點已逐漸由評價擴張轉向企業獲利成長與基本面表現。短期市場仍須留意
政策面不確定性 ， 美中貿易談判進展將牽動全球供應鏈與科技產業展望 ， 若關稅或科技限制措施再度
升溫，可能對科技股評價造成壓力；此外，美國期中選舉逐漸接近，企業稅率、科技監管及產業政策
等議題亦可能影響市場風險偏好。整體而言，企業獲利改善仍是支撐美股最重要的力量，若 AI 投資
循環與獲利成長趨勢延續，美股中長期表現仍可望由基本面驅動。

歐洲股市：儘管企業獲利上修，仍需持續關注地緣政治發展
9 月泛歐 600 指數下跌約 2%，產業表現方面 ，製藥 、生技、銀行及部分科技類股表現相對強勢，
顯示資金偏好具獲利能見度與防禦特性的族群 ； 相較之下 ， 礦業 、 消費及部分金融服務類股表現落後 ，
反映市場對歐洲經濟成長及企業獲利前景仍持審慎態度。整體而言，受到高油價以及高利率環境，投
資氣氛轉趨保守，市場資金持續聚焦於基本面較穩健的產業。
歐洲央行 9 月政策利率由 2.25% 升息 1 碼至 2.50 % ，符合市場預期。歐洲央行最新經濟預
測中上修中期通膨展望，2027 年消費者物價指數年增率由 2.3% 升至 2.5% ，2028 年則由 2.0%

上調至 2.1% ；核心消費者物價指數方面，2027 年和 2028 年預測亦各上修 0.1 % 至 2.6% 及
2.3% ，能源成本上升對食品及其他價格的傳導，使得通膨回落速度較原先預期緩慢。ECB 總裁拉加
德表示能源價格上漲逐步傳導至核心及食品通膨，預期整體通膨在 2027 年上半年維持在高檔。
根據花旗歐洲獲利修正指數(Earnings Revision Index) ，歐洲企業獲利預估已連續上修並到近五
年高點，反映企業基本面持續改善。受惠於歐元區景氣穩步復甦、製造業景氣回升，以及企業資本支
出逐步回暖，工業、金融及景氣循環類股獲利展望同步改善。隨著市場對企業未來盈餘預期提高，有
助支撐歐股評價面與資金流入，並成為推動泛歐 600 指數表現的重要動能。
然而，自 3 月美伊衝突爆發以來，市場對能源供給中斷的擔憂持續升溫，帶動油價走高，進而增
加仰賴進口能源的歐洲企業成本壓力，並壓抑歐股相對表現。進入9 月後，俄羅斯與北約緊張關係再
度升溫，歐洲安全局勢不確定性提高，進一步加劇市場對能源供應與地緣政治風險的擔憂。若相關衝

突持續延燒，不僅可能推升能源價格與通膨壓力，也可能影響企業投資意願及市場風險偏好，進而對
歐洲企業獲利與股市表現形成壓力。
展望 10 月，歐股仍將受到高油價、地緣政治風險及歐洲央行維持偏鷹立場等因素影響，市場波
動性可能維持偏高。近期能源價格攀升推升通膨預期，使 ECB 傾向維持較高利率水準，不僅提高企
業融資成本，也限制股市評價進一步擴張的空間。此外，中東局勢持續緊張，以及俄羅斯與北約間的
緊張關係再度升高，均可能影響投資人風險偏好，增添歐洲金融市場不確定性。雖然歐元區經濟活動
仍維持溫和擴張，企業獲利預估亦持續改善，但在高利率與地緣政治風險尚未明顯緩解之前，市場情
緒仍偏向審慎，後續仍需持續關注能源價格、通膨走勢及地緣政治發展，作為判斷歐股能否重啟上漲
動能的重要觀察指標。

日本股市：升息落地，景氣展望佳，第四季半導體與商社企業獲利動能有望延續
9 月日本股市受升息預期及長天期公債殖利率走高影響，部分高評價股票出現震盪，但這波調整
較偏向政策轉折下的評價重估，而非企業基本面全面轉弱。日本銀行於 9 月 18 日決議將短期政策利
率目標提高至約 1.25%，使市場反覆討論的升息方向逐漸明朗。隨著政策落地，投資焦點有望由利率
變動轉回企業營運與獲利。從正面角度看，利率正常化反映日本經濟具備一定韌性，股價整理也有助
於消化先前累積的評價壓力。若後續企業財測保持穩健，9 月的震盪可望成為第四季行情重新蓄勢的

過程。
日本股市的中期支撐，來自薪資成長、內需改善與企業投資的相互帶動。2026 年春鬥最終統計
顯示，5,368 個工會的平均調薪幅度達 5.01%，連續第三年維持在 5%以上；可明確辨識的基本薪資
調升幅度亦達 3.50%。薪資持續增加，有助於提升家庭消費能力，為零售與服務等內需產業提供較穩
定的需求基礎。若企業能透過產品競爭力、定價能力及經營效率吸收成本，消費回溫便有機會轉化為
獲利成長。同時，全球 AI 相關投資也為日本半導體設備、載板及電子零組件供應鏈創造機會，使內
需復甦與科技需求形成雙重支撐。
展望 10 月，隨著升息議題逐步由預期轉為已知條件，市場可望更加重視第四季訂單、企業財測
及實際獲利。科技產業方面，若全球 AI 相關資本支出持續擴大，日本具技術優勢的設備與零組件企
業，有機會將需求轉化為接單及出貨成長；商社與資源相關企業，則可觀察能源、原物料業務及投資
收益對現金流的貢獻。內需產業亦值得留意薪資成長能否帶動消費，以及企業能否維持利潤率。整體
而言，10 月的投資機會不必侷限於單一題材，市場有望逐步從政策消息主導，轉向由產業需求與企

業業績支撐的行情。
投資策略方面，半導體供應鏈可優先挑選訂單能見度高、具技術門檻且獲利成長明確的企業；商
社則著重現金流、資本配置與股東回饋，內需股則觀察消費回溫能否反映在營收及利潤。若股價因利
率或匯率波動而短暫回落，但企業基本面與財測未明顯改變，可考慮分批布局，避免追逐短線漲幅。
後續仍須追蹤長天期利率 、 日圓及企業財測變化 ， 但投資主軸可更積極地放在具備實質成長能力的公
司。日本央行升息落地後，企業獲利能否接棒，將是日本股市延續表現的關鍵。

台灣股市：訂單持續暢旺突破千億，AI 需求持續向供應鏈擴散，廠商展望樂觀
9 月觀察台股基本面，最明確的支撐來自 8 月外銷訂單創下單月新高，以及企業對 9 月接單維持
擴張預期。經濟部公布，8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，月增 5.1%、年增 71.4%；其中資訊通信
與電子產品合計 799.6 億美元，占整體 77.7%，顯示 AI 伺服器、半導體與雲端建設仍是主要成長來
源。受查廠商對 9 月接單的預期，以訂單金額計算的動向指數為 52.1，高於代表持平的 50，意味整
體接單有望較 8 月增加。雖然 9 月實際結果仍待公布，但已發布的數據與企業展望，為台股進入第

四季提供了正面的基本面依據。
更值得留意的是，AI 需求正從核心晶片延伸至更廣的供應鏈。雲端業者擴充運算能力，除了帶動
伺服器與半導體接單，也使自研 ASIC、IC 設計及相關電子零組件成為後續觀察重點；半導體投資與
設備擴產 ， 則為自動化設備 、 銅箔及高階材料創造機會 。8 月機械與基本金屬外銷訂單分別年增 28.3%
及 26.8%，顯示科技投資的效益已反映在部分非電子貨品。對台股而言，這有助於投資主線從少數大
型權值股，延伸至具技術優勢與訂單能見度的中上游企業。不過，面板需求仍偏弱，產業表現並非全
面同步，選股仍須辨別企業實際受惠程度。
展望 10 月，市場焦點可望轉向第三季財報、第四季營運展望，以及 9 月外銷訂單能否驗證接單
續強。就廠商對 9 月的預期而言，資訊通信與電子產品的訂單金額動向指數分別為 52.4 及 51.5，顯
示科技需求仍具支撐；基本金屬與化學品則達 59.0 及 61.0，呈現部分材料領域接單改善的期待。若
AI 伺服器出貨 、 自研晶片開發及半導體資本支出持續推進 ， 相關台灣供應鏈的營收與獲利有望受惠。

另一方面，機械與光學器材指數分別為 46.3 及 49.5，提醒市場不能將整體訂單成長視為所有產業同
步復甦，仍須以後續實績檢驗。
投資策略方面，10 月以「掌握 AI 主線、尋找擴散機會、重視獲利驗證」作為配置方向。核心部
位可留意伺服器、半導體及 IC 設計等需求較明確的企業，並觀察 ASIC 相關業務能否由開發逐步轉
化為穩定出貨；其他機會則可從設備、銅箔與高階材料中，篩選確實取得訂單的公司。選股不只看產
業接單增幅，更應比較個別企業的營收成長、毛利率與獲利展望，避免在評價已高時追逐短期題材。
整體而言，台股仍有基本面支撐，後續行情能否擴大，關鍵在於 AI 需求能否持續轉化為更多企業的
實際獲利。

中國股市：AI 產業規模擴張難掩獲利困境，產業進入變現考驗
9 月中國 AI 產業的討論，從模型能力與使用量，逐漸轉向更根本的問題：在高階晶片取得受限、
業者競爭激烈的環境下，龐大的投入能否形成可持續的回報。9 月中美高層會談後，雙方宣布建立
AI 相關對話機制，但公開成果未顯示高階 AI 晶片出口限制已放寬。政策溝通有所進展，並未立即改
變企業取得先進算力的條件 。 與此同時 ， 低價及開放權重策略持續降低AI 使用門檻 ， 帶動市場擴張，
也使企業更難單靠模型服務取得理想利潤。對投資人而言，9 月最重要的訊號不是單一產品的表現，

而是中國 AI 已進入需要同時回答算力效率、商業變現與資本回收的階段。
從整體結構來看，中國具備電力、製造基礎及廣泛應用場景，有利於 AI 以較低成本進入企業營
運與日常服務；但部署成本較低，不代表產業獲利便能同步提高。當業者以低價爭取用戶，使用量可
以迅速累積，收入卻可能跟不上基礎設施、研發及營運支出。以特定平台的觀察為例，DeepSeek 曾
取得可觀的代幣使用量，但相應支出占比明顯較低；這不能代表中國 AI 產業的整體財務表現，卻凸
顯「有人使用」與「願意付費」之間仍有距離。未來產業能否改善獲利，取決於企業是否找到具持續
需求的應用、建立合理收費方式，並避免同質化服務陷入反覆降價。
展望 10 月 ， 中國AI 的成長空間仍在 ， 但市場衡量進展的標準應由使用規模 ， 逐步轉向投入產出。
若 AI 更深入企業工作流程，相關需求有望延續；然而，需求增加後，業者仍須證明服務品質、運算
效率與客戶付費能同步改善 。 晶片限制使算力使用效率更加重要 ， 價格競爭則使收入品質成為另一項
考驗。Vercel 資料顯示，8 月開放權重模型在其平台占代幣使用量 56%，支出占比卻為 14%，說明

高使用量未必對應高支出；10 月宜觀察企業是否開始披露更清楚的付費需求與成本改善，而非只比
較流量或模型排名。
投資策略方面，對中國 AI 宜維持關注成長、審慎評估獲利的態度，避免把產業滲透率提高，直
接等同於所有企業的投資價值提升。評估重點可放在三個整體問題：第一，持續增加的算力與研發投
入，能否換來足夠的收入；第二，低價競爭下，企業能否留住付費客戶並維持合理利潤；第三，AI
應用能否從試用走向長期採購。具備穩定需求、成本控制能力及清楚收費模式的企業，較有機會在產
業整合中建立優勢。整體而言，中國 AI 並非缺少使用場景，而是仍須證明大規模使用可以轉化為可
持續的獲利，這將是 10 月觀察產業與相關市場表現的核心。

科技類股：AI 應用加速落地，光通訊與 Agent 入口迎來成長機會
AI 產業的成長主軸正由模型訓練延伸至推論與實際應用 ， 讓軟體服務與硬體建設形成相互帶動的
循環。當 AI 不再只回答問題，而能協助搜尋資訊、安排活動及完成跨平台任務，使用頻率與運算需
求有望提高。Meta 於 9 月推出個人 AI 助理 Muse，透過應用程式及 WhatsApp 接觸既有用戶，積
極爭取 AI 服務入口。對產業而言，Agent 普及不僅意味新的服務形式，也可能擴大雲端運算與資料
傳輸需求，使應用端的創新成為基礎設施升級的推力。投資機會可從使用者入口延伸至相關供應鏈。

在硬體端，AI 推論與多步驟任務若持續增加，資料中心內部的資料交換將更頻繁，高速且節能的
連接能力因而更具價值 。 光通訊除支援資料中心之間的傳輸 ， 也逐步朝機櫃及晶片附近的互連場景發
展 ， 拓展供應商的產品空間 。Lumentum 指出 ，AI 運算對速度與頻寬的要求提升 ， 帶動光連結需求，
並已在營收展望中反映相關成長 。 高速光模組與新型互連方案也持續推進 。 雖然部分新技術仍須經過
客戶導入與量產驗證，但既有產品需求與後續技術升級有望接續，讓光通訊成為 AI 基礎設施中值得
持續關注的環節。
在應用端，Muse 代表 AI 從被動回覆走向主動協助執行任務的嘗試。若 Agent 能替使用者完成
搜尋、比較、預訂與付款等流程，便利性提升有望促進使用黏著度，並為支付、交易與服務串接平台
創造新的合作機會。跨平台操作可能由單一入口整合，促使各產業重新思考客戶服務。 「Muse 對各
產業的影響」圖可呈現商業模式轉變的可能方向。隨著更多服務接入 Agent 生態系，使用情境有望
持續擴展；後續若任務完成率、使用留存及付費轉換逐步改善，應用端便有機會形成更明確的收入來

源，並進一步支持運算與傳輸投資。
投資策略方面，建議以「應用入口與基礎設施並重」掌握 AI 從訓練走向推論的成長機會。硬體
方面，可優先關注已切入主要客戶供應鏈、具備高速光模組與新型互連產品布局，且訂單能見度較佳
的業者。應用方面，則可追蹤具備用戶基礎、服務整合能力與清楚收費模式的平台，觀察 Agent 能
否從新產品熱度轉化為持續使用與付費需求 。 配置上可先以業績較明確的基礎設施業者為核心 ， 再逐
步增加應用平台的布局，兼顧當前需求與後續成長空間。整體而言，AI 應用愈深入日常生活與企業
流程 ， 入口平台與光通訊供應鏈愈有機會相互受惠 ； 選股關鍵仍是找出能將需求成長轉化為實際業績
的企業。

拉美市場：10 月巴西總統大選將登場，政治不確定性影響市場表現
9 月拉美市場維持震盪整理 。 一方面 ， 拉美各國通膨數據普遍低於市場預期 ，有利市場降息預期；
另一方面，巴西總統大選選情持續拉鋸，政治不確定性升溫則讓投資人保持觀望。展望 10 月，隨巴
西大選投票日逼近，政治因素仍可能使拉美市場短線波動加劇。
受到美伊衝突帶動油價上漲影響，市場原本擔心燃料與運輸成本增加，會讓拉美物價再度大幅上
漲，進而迫使拉美央行升息。然而，高盛分析指出，拉美市場目前的通膨率仍受到控制，當地經濟承
受能源漲價的能力 ， 比市場原先擔心的情況更好 。 其中一個重要原因是 ， 新興市場政府透過燃油補貼 、

稅制安排與零售價格管制，讓消費者實際感受到的價格漲幅相對有限。此外，上半年全球農產品供給
較為充裕，使食品價格漲幅偏低，也抵銷了部分能源漲價帶來的影響。由於拉美市場實際通膨壓力小
於原先市場預期，央行進一步大幅升息的必要性也隨之降低，高盛因此認為，市場目前對多數拉美經
濟體未來一至兩年的升息預期過於悲觀，並預期拉美各國央行在 2027 年較可能重新轉向降息。
不過，拉美多國經濟出現放緩跡象，亦可能壓抑股市上漲空間。智利央行於 9 月會議維持政策利
率 4.50%不變 ， 並將2026 年經濟成長預測下修至 0.25%至 0.75%， 低於6 月預估的 1.0%至 1.75%。
智利央行指出，第二季內需成長放緩，勞動市場及信心指標同步轉弱，加上燃料價格上漲侵蝕企業與
家庭所得，均對經濟活動形成壓力。巴西經濟亦出現放緩跡象，巴西央行於 9 月 16 日一致決議調降
Selic 政策利率 25 個基點至 13.75%，並指出景氣循環產業動能轉弱，且整體與核心通膨均已回落至
目標區間上限以下，為進一步降息提供空間。不過，巴西央行並未承諾下次會議是否將繼續降息，而

是強調將視未來經濟與通膨數據逐次評估。
另一方面，巴西總統大選進入白熱化階段則是當前影響市場最主要的因子。市場民調顯示，相較
7 月時現任總統魯拉在民調中普遍領先競爭者波索納洛約 5 至 8 個百分點 ，在 9 月的民調雙方差距已
縮小至 2 個百分點以內，選情不確定性仍高。若選舉最終由魯拉勝選，其支持財政擴張的政策，將可
能使市場對於巴西財政赤字問題的擔憂加劇，進而使巴西股市面臨較大壓力；而若波索納洛勝選，市
場普遍預期其能提出改善投資人信心的財政政策 ， 為巴西央行創造更多降息空間 ， 有利後續巴西股市
上行。
整體而言，儘管市場對拉美 各國當前升息預期可能過於悲觀，但目前拉美多國經濟成長同步放
緩，加上巴西總統大選選情膠著，選後財政政策方向不確定性高，在考量大選及政策方向不確定性的
狀況下，短線拉美市場波動恐將加劇。

能源類股：全球石油庫存持續下降，但中東石油出口已出現回升
9 月西德州原油期貨價格受美伊衝突升溫影響，一度於月中重新站上每桶 100 美元，其後隨衝突
趨緩，油價漲幅逐步收斂，月底約回落至90 美元附近，能源類股表現亦大致跟隨油價走勢。展望10
月，近期中東石油供應恢復速度不如預期，加上庫存持續下降，預期油價下檔仍具支撐。然而，市場
數據顯示，高油價及供應受阻已同步抑制石油需求，且中東各國原油出口持續回升，後續供應緊張情
況有望逐步改善。因此，雖然中東地緣政治風險仍將對油價形成支撐，但隨美伊重啟外交接觸，油價
進一步大幅上漲的空間預期相對有限。

受到美伊談判僵持影響，國際能源署（IEA）與美國能源資訊署（EIA）在 9 月月報均將中東石油
供應恢復的預期時間延後。IEA 將中東石油供應全面恢復的預期時間延至 2027 年，並將 2026 年全
球石油供應減幅，由上月預估的每日減少 430 萬桶擴大至 570 萬桶；EIA 則將中東石油供給恢復至
衝突前水準的時間，由 2027 年初延至第 2 季。
此外 ，受到中東供應受阻與油價偏高影響 ，IEA 亦將 2026 年全球石油需求預測下修每日 94 萬桶 ，
預估全年需求將較去年減少每日 250 萬桶，EIA 則預估全年石油需求將減少約每日 170 萬桶，石油
輸出國組織（OPEC）也將今年需求由上月預估的每日增加 60 萬桶下修至 40 萬桶。
受此影響，全球石油庫存持續下降。IEA 估計，8 月全球可觀察石油庫存減少 9,500 萬桶，自 2
月底以來已累計下降 5.07 億桶，顯示市場仍持續動用庫存填補缺口。EIA 也預期全球石油庫存將持
續下降至年底，因此將2026 年下半年布蘭特原油均價預測，由上月約每桶82 美元上調至 90 美元；

此外 ，隨著 2027 年中東出口與產量逐步恢復 ，IEA 預估明年全年布蘭特原油均價將回落至 74 美元。
另一方面，中東原油出口則已經出現回升跡象。路透社 9 月 28 日引述 Kpler 初步資料指出，沙
烏地阿拉伯與阿聯酋 9 月原油出口均出現上升 ， 帶動中東主要產油國當月原油日均出口量回升至每日
1,280 萬桶，創下 2 月戰爭爆發以來最高水準。其中，沙烏地阿拉伯9 月原油日均出口預估將達每日
540 萬桶，較 8 月的 244.6 萬桶增加逾一倍。由於沙國東西向輸油管線先前因攻擊受損，沙烏地計
畫將部分原先經紅海延布港出口的原油 ， 轉往波斯灣沿岸港口裝運 ， 帶動拉斯坦努拉港9 月日均出貨
量預估由 8 月的每日 92.9 萬桶增至 360 萬桶。
整體而言，中東供應恢復時程延後，加上全球石油庫存持續下降，短期仍有利支撐油價。然而，
石油需求預估同步下修 ， 且沙烏地阿拉伯與阿聯酋原油出口均已逐步回升 ，後續石油供應緊張程度可
望有所緩解。整體而言，儘管油價仍受中東地緣風險支撐，但隨美國期中選舉接近，近期美伊亦重啟

外交接觸，市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂可望逐步降溫，油價進一步大幅上漲空間相對有限。

身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，

黃金類股：升息預期攀升壓抑金價表現；財富配置及央行買盤支撐中長期需求
近期黃金價格表現較為疲弱，主要受到市場升息預期持續攀升，帶動美債殖利率走高，增加持有
黃金的機會成本，對於利率較為敏感的黃金 ETF 需求因此受到壓抑。然而，高盛研究指出，聯準會
近期決議升息雖然延長了市場高利率環境時間，但並不會進一步墊高利率終點 ， 加上全球央行持續購
金，以及投資人從財富配置角度增加黃金配置，均有助於支撐中期黃金需求。
高盛報告指出，聯準會於 9 月升息一碼，增加持有黃金的機會成本，使對利率敏感的黃金ETF 需
求下滑 ， 高盛因此將2026 年底金價預估由每盎司 4,900 美元下修至 4,650 美元 。 然而 ， 高盛也預估，

聯準會將於 2027 年 9 月至 2028 年 3 月期間降息三碼，使政策利率最終回到 3.25%至 3.5%區間，
故儘管聯準會 9 月升息延長了高利率環境，但實際並未墊高利率終點，因此來自黃金 ETF 的賣壓僅
會延後金價上漲時程，但並未改變高盛對黃金的偏多看法。
另一方面，央行買盤仍是金價支撐核心。高盛9 月報告預估，全球央行近三個月經季節調整後的
購金速度已上升至每月 91 噸，遠高於 2022 年前每月 17 噸的平均水準。高盛指出，這股需求主要
來自分散外匯儲備的考量，在 2022 年俄羅斯央行資產遭美國凍結後，各國央行更加重視儲備資產遭
制裁或凍結的風險，相較於對利率敏感的 ETF 資金，央行基於儲備分散而進行的購金，不易因短期
利率變動而轉向，因此即使聯準會升息，央行買盤仍有利支撐金價表現。
除了央行買盤外，花旗也從全球財富配置的角度看好黃金。花旗指出，全球每年新增黃金礦產供
應規模相當有限，若全球家庭額外將 0.1%的財富配置於黃金，就相當於約 4,459 噸黃金購買量，也

等於 1.2 年的新增礦產供應量。換言之，即使全球投資人僅小幅提高黃金配置比重，也可能創造遠超
過新增供給的需求。由於黃金供給短期內難以快速增加，市場往往需要透過金價上漲，吸引既有持有
人釋出黃金以滿足新增需求，因此資產配置需求增加可能對金價產生顯著推升影響。
不過，花旗也指出，黃金新增供給有限的特性雖有助於放大需求對金價的推升效果，但同時也可
能提高金價出現較劇烈修正的風險。花旗估計，2022 年以來的金價上漲已使既有黃金持有人的帳面
財富增加約 13 兆美元，若持有人將其中約 1 兆美元轉回股票、債券或現金，所形成的黃金賣壓便可
能超過目前全部實體黃金需求，故當持有人集中獲利了結時，金價也可能出現較劇烈的修正。
整體而言，在市場升息預期持續攀升的情況下，短期黃金ETF 需求可能受到壓抑，金價仍可能面
臨震盪 。 然而 ， 全球央行基於外匯儲備分散需求持續購金 ， 加上投資人從財富配置角度提高黃金配置 ，
均有助於支撐中期黃金需求。

### Page 11

身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：聯準會升息穩定通膨預期，凸顯中天期美債與投資級債吸引力
美國 9 月聯準會貨幣政策會議以 12 比 0 全票通過 ， 將聯邦基金利率目標區間上調 25 個基點至
3.75%–4.00%。最新經濟預測同步將 2026 年核心 PCE 上修至 3.4%、GDP 成長率上修至 2.3%，且
全面上修 2026 至 2027 年政策利率預估至 4.1%，並暗示年底前仍有一次升息空間。9 月聯準會採取
預防性升息，主要是在避免通膨預期鬆動並維持政策公信力，確立高利率維持更久的政策基調。
美國政府於 9 月 30 日公布第二季國內生產毛額(GDP)終值，經季節調整後成長年率由 1.5%上修

至 2.2%，高於經濟學家預估的維持不變。第一季 GDP 季增年率也由 2.1%上修至 2.5%。其中，占
美國經濟活動逾 6 成的消費支出，第二季成長率由 3.4%上修至 3.8%，顯示美國消費動能強勁；元
大投顧指出，美國 8 月核心資本財訂單月增率加速至 1.6%，其中初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均
呈現增長，顯示 AI 相關投資及企業設備更新需求持續支撐資本財訂單，企業設備投資可望對經濟成
長形成穩健貢獻。在消費與企業動能表現強勁下，使市場重新評估美國經濟對通膨黏性的影響。
彭博 10 月 1 日的報告指出，在 Market Pulse 調查的 173 名受訪者，超過一半的人預測美國 30
年期公債殖利率在年底將達到 6%。儘管伊朗戰爭和高借貸成本持續影響 ， 美國經濟仍維持強勁成長 ，
這種韌性加劇了通膨和進一步升息的⾵險；人工智慧基礎設施建設競賽引發了企業發債的狂潮 ，推升
長天期債券發行量，全球企業今年以來已發行超過 4000 億美元的債券，用於技術投資，而其中大部
分債券發行於美國。另外，各國政府持續的預算赤字，美國聯邦政府債務規模首次突破 40 兆美元，

美國政府需要擴大發債量 ， 投資人要求更高的利率來購買債券等因素 ，皆為推升美國長天期殖利率上
行的原因。因此，長天期債券後續表現仍須持續觀察利率走勢、債券供需變化及通膨發展。
展望 10 月，由於美國通膨仍高於聯準會 2%的目標，2026 年底有再升息一碼的可能，使得短
天期利率下行空間有限。在政策利率可能維持高檔、長天期殖利率又受到供給面壓力推升的環境下，
當前的債券配置應以「息收防禦」與「存續期間控管」為核心。隨著美國公債利率上行，彭博美國投
資級債指數平均剩餘到期期限持續下降 ， 存續期間縮短有助降低利率變動對債券價格的影響 。美國債
市投資建議重點配置中天期美國公債與投資級公司債 。中天期美國公債與投資級公司債不僅能鎖定具
吸引力的收益率水準，也能兼顧存續期間風險控管，降低利率波動對投資組合淨值的衝擊，在當前環
境下有助於兼顧收益與風險控制。

在完成客戶 5,000 小時可靠度測試認證後，光環代工業務自 26H2
起重新貢獻營運， 隨稼動率提升及產品組合優化， 本業獲利已顯著
改善並順利轉虧為盈 。 為配合客戶後續產能規劃， 公司亦將斥資約
11 億元進行產線擴充，預期至2027 年底產能規模將達 2026 年底
的 3 倍。此外，針對具 Scale-up 應用潛力的 VCSEL 技術，光環
亦規劃推出 1060nm VCSEL 方案供客戶驗證，有望切入新一代
AI 光互連市場，進一步強化公司中長期競爭優勢。綜合以上，研
究部預估光環 2026 年 EPS 0.57 元，2027 年 EPS 6.77 元，目前
PE 為 26.5x，參考光通上游同業 PE 評價為 20-60x，考量光環本
業獲利將開始由虧轉盈且顯著成長，且也開始佈局 Scale-up 雷射
方案，因此給予 35xPER，目標價 240 元，建議買進。

換件 28%、其他 9%。應用領域則為頻寬網路 10%、4G/5G 基地
台互聯 31%、雲端數據中心 22%、感測應用 26%、其他 11%，銷
售地區為台灣 60%、 中國8%、 歐洲及美洲17%、 亞洲及其他15%。

事件 
2026年9月，CPI同比增速0.9%（市场预期1%），前值0.8%， 环比增速0.2%， 前值0.4%，
1-9月CPI累计同比0.9%；PPI同比增速5%（市场预期4.4%） ， 较上月提高1.2个百分点，
环比增速1.2%，前值0.4%，1-9月PPI累计同比2.3%。 
1    非食品项改善支撑 CPI 增速，能源相关分项较强 
CPI同比增速延续回升，非食品项持续改善，为主要支撑项。9月，CPI同比增速由上
月的0.8%回升至0.9%，略低于市场预期，非食品项为主要支撑因素。其中，CPI食品
项同比增速由上月的-1.4%回落至-1.7%，环比持平于0.4%。非食品项同比由1.2%升至
1.5%，环比增速由上月的0.3%小幅下行至0.2%。 
图表1：CPI同比增速延续回升（%） 图表2：CPI食品项环比处于历史同期较弱水平（%）

资料来源：iFind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：iFind，万联证券研究所 
核心CPI、 服务项和消费品同比增速持平， 环比增速下滑。核心CPI同比增速持平于1%，
环比0%， 前值0.1%。CPI服务项同比持平于0.8%， 环比-0.2%， 前值0.1%； 消费品同比
增速则由0.8%升至1%，环比0.5%，前值0.6%。

证券研究报告|宏观报告

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 4 页 共 8 页  
猪肉价格转为下跌，鲜菜价格弱于季节性，鲜果延续季节性回落。9月猪肉价格环比
转跌，部分原因是集团猪企为规避节后风险在节前集中出栏， 散户跟随行动， 另一方
面， 双节备货力度不及预期， 导致供应增速高于需求增速，出现“旺季不旺” 的现象。
预计后续随着产能去化效果显现， 猪肉价格将逐步企稳。牛羊肉价格延续上涨。鲜菜
价格下跌，弱于季节性；鲜果价格则延续季节性回落。 
从CPI食品项具体分项来看，9月，猪肉价格同比增速继续回升1.8个百分点至-10%，
下跌速度减缓， 环比增速由上月的1.3%小幅回落至1.2%；牛、羊肉同比增速则分别由
上月的5.3%、6.3%上行至7.5%、6.4%。水产品同比由-0.6%升至0.4%，蛋类同比增速
由15%回落至14.3%，环比2%。鲜菜价格同比增速由-2.8%降至-8.1%，环比降至0.5%；
鲜果价格同比由-0.5%回落至-1.5%，环比由负转正， 升至0.6%。粮食、 食用油、 烟草、
酒类同比增速分别由8月的-0.6%、-1.3%、-0.7%、-2.9%变动为-0.6%、-1.1%、-0.8%、
-3.1%。

燃料同比由8.3%继续升至14.9%，环比4.5%，受国际油价高企带动；水电燃料同比增

2    国际油价带动 PPI 走高，AI 产业维持高增速 
PPI同比升至2022年以来新高，生产资料为主要拉动，耐用消费品增幅扩大。国际原
油价格高企，输入性通胀压力明显，PPI同、环比均上行，其中同比增速录得2022年
以来新高。PPIRM同比增速上升，幅度与PPI接近。PPI生产资料为主要拉动项，其中
原料工业、 采掘工业、 加工工业涨幅均扩大， 加工工业与原材料工业剪刀差走阔。生
活资料同比跌幅继续收窄， 其中衣着跌幅持平，食品、一般日用品收窄，耐用消费品
增速延续上行。 
具体数据来看，PPI同比5%，较上月上行1.2%个百分点，高于市场预期的4.4%，环比
增速1.2%，前值0.4%。工业生产者购进价格指数（PPIRM）同比由5.8%上行至6.9%，
环比1.2%， 前值0.3%。PPI生产资料同比增速上升1.5个百分点至6.5%， 其中， 采掘工
业、 原料工业和加工工业同比增速分别由上月的17.8%、6.7%、3.1%变动至25.4%、9.4%、
3.4%。PPI生活资料同比跌幅收窄0.4个百分点至-0.1%，环比增速0.1%，前值0.2%；
其中， 衣着同比跌幅维持在-1.2%， 食品同比跌幅收窄至-1.8%，一般日用品跌幅缩小
至-0.6%，耐用消费品同比由1.2%升至1.6%。

国际油价高企带动能化产业链通胀升温，黑色金属产业价格表现较弱。采掘业分项同
比增速分化，环比普遍上行，其中石油和天然气开采业同比增速大幅上行，从10.5%
上行至24.5%， 环比增速10.4%。 黑色金属矿采选业同比增速下行， 由1.1%回落至-3.4%，
环比跌幅小幅收窄； 有色金属矿采选业同比增速小幅上行0.3个百分点至21.4%， 环比
由负转正。 煤炭开采和洗选业同比由26.6%上行至35.3%， 环比9.5%。 中游加工制造业
通胀升温， 石油、 煤炭及其他燃料加工业同比从11.1%升至21.6%， 化学原料和化学制
品制造业同比增速上行3个百分点至12.1%，化学纤维制造业同比上行2.7个百分点至
12.3%。下游加工业中有色金属冶炼表现较强，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业同比由 -
0.3%微升至-0.2%，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业同比增速上行0.6个百分点至21.4%。
非金属矿物制品业同比跌幅收窄0.7个百分点至-2.5%，环比0.3%。 
下游高端制造业价格走势分化， 汽车跌幅收窄，AI产业维持强势。下游汽车制造业同
比跌幅收窄0.2个百分点至-2%，环比-0.2%。铁路、船舶等运输设备制造业同比持平
于0.1%，环比0。计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业维持强势，同比由5.3%进一步
升至5.8%， 环比0.4%， 前值0.6%。AI、 算电协同加快发展带动相关行业需求增加， 计
算机通信和其他电子设备制造业、电气机械和器材制造业合计拉动 PPI环比增速约
0.07个百分点。电力、 热力生产和供应业同比跌幅扩大、 燃气生产和供应业增速上行，
水的生产和供应业增速持平。

美联储在明年 1 ⽉底前累计加息约 40 个基点， 10 ⽉采取⾏动的概率亦⼩幅上升。
摩根⼤通资产管理⾸席全球策略师 David Kelly 表⽰，现在加息 " ⼤概率是愚蠢之举 " ，并明确表⽰
不预期联储在 10 ⽉会议上采取⾏动。与此同时，市场正密切关注美联储主席 Kevin Warsh 周五在
曼⾕的讲话安排。

表⽰， " 美国 CPI 超预期与 CoreWeave 债券抛售，正促使投资者重新评估整个 AI 板块的融资⻛

险。 "

⾸席策略师 Hiroshi Namioka 表⽰， " 投资者⽬前正在削减⻛险，尽可能将资⾦转移⾄避险资产，

债负相关性正在重新确⽴，避险资⾦向债市回流的迹象明显。
Anthropic 的最新数据在此时显得格外引⼈关注。据知情⼈⼠透露，该公司第三季度营收达 168 亿
美元，部分分析师认为该数字低于预期。 Vantage Global Prime ⾼级市场分析师 Hebe Chen 表
⽰， " 市场对 Anthropic 亮眼数据的反应并⾮失望，⽽是对维持这⼀增⻓所需副作⽤的⽇益担忧
—— 在流动性本已趋紧的市场中， AI 将继续消耗⼤量资本，包括 Anthropic 即将推进的 IPO 。 "

来⾼点。⻩⾦在连跌三⽇后出现反弹，报每盎司约 4140 美元。全球原油基准布伦特原油下跌

持续紧张带动的油价⾼企，被视为推升美国通胀的重要因素之⼀，亦是本轮市场波动的深层背
景。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

and plans to double annual capacity from about 1GW in 2025, split across its California

and Delaware sites, to 2GW by the end of 2026; and will further expand the capacity
to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our channel checks.

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

High-speed products a new driver
In 3Q24, ACES passed product qualification with ODMs for the high-speed
connectors used on Nvidia GB200 networking cards (NICs) and accelerator cards,
marking its move from traditional PC peripherals into the server space and Nvidia
supply chain. In 2025, server revenue accounted for 10% of ACES’s total revenue.
Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s server business to grow 107% in 2026, thanks to

its new product offerings, higher content per rack of high-speed products, and
strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.
Figure 13 Figure 14
ACES high-speed connector content per rack increase ACES provides MCIO and a wide range of high-speed connectors

Source: SinoPac Source: ACES
Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

### Page 12

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 12
Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Figure 29 Figure 30
ACES Industrial product portfolio Industrial sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

## Document 278: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

Exhibit 1: Rising yields and uncertainty have increased the cost of capital 
30-year government bond benchmark yields (%)

### Page 2

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

reflected in other regions too. Today, a 2-standard deviation move in 10-year Treasury 
yields would equate to about 50bps over a month or 30bps over two weeks, similar to 
the adjustments in recent days which explains the pullback in equity prices.

4. But despite the rise in yields, nominal GDP and profit growth remain strong and, until 
recently, the impact of rising bond yields has been offset by the strength in corporate 
profits. Earnings growth has been the main driver of equity returns over the past 18 
months in all regions (Exhibit 5). This strength has meant that PE multiples have either 
been flat (Japan and Europe) or have fallen (the US, Asia and EM).

In the US, in particular, the forward PE multiple for the S&P has come down from 22x at

### Page 4

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

Exhibit 6: US equities have underperformed other major regions since 2025

### Page 5

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

The combination of higher cost of capital and greater capital intensity in the tech sector 
has reduced the value of their future cash flows and triggered a de-rating in the biggest 
companies.  The dominant capex hyperscalers in the US now have a forward PE close to 
the average of the rest of the market.

8. Software companies have also de-rated on the back of fears of disruption from AI 
while memory and chips stocks have also experienced lower multiples as their earnings
